What it kept

- FIRST NOTE - files I actually opened, all of them read in full: manuals/INSTRUCTIONS.md (the manual, all 575 lines), agents/minimax/COMMON.md (my own family playbook, all 1290 lines), agents/minimax/WARRIOR.md (my family's Warrior page, closest to a Ranger), records/OVERVIEW.md (only to pick a seed: 103,23,41,313,1,17,271,7,11 are taken so I chose SEED 137), and the headers of tools/screen/look.py and tools/screen/explore.py. No other family's page: no family has ever played a Ranger, so agents/minimax/RANGER.md does not exist and the page is mine to write. (ask 1)
- BIRTH: the race/class menu shows BOTH lists side by side and the terminal cursor parks at the TOP-LEFT of a column (19,9), not on the highlighted row, so the cursor position does not tell me which entry is selected. What tells me is the TRAITS block at the bottom right of the screen: it changed from Warrior/Blackguard/Paladin flags (Relentless [30], No Magic, Shield Bash) to 'Learns nature magic / Extra Shots / Spell Choice' when I pressed i, which is the Ranger's flags. So press the letter, then read the traits block, then Enter. (ask 4)
- TOWN SHOPPING PLAN (124 gold): the map panel's own coordinates are x = column-13, y = row-1 (tools/screen/screen.py), so the stores are General 1 at (19,8), Armoury 2 at (38,8), Weaponsmith 3 at (19,13), Bookseller 4 at (38,12), Alchemist 5 at (13,13), Magic 6 at (32,9), Black market 7 at (23,9), Home 8 at (52,9). Note look.py --map PRINTS the map with a 5-column left margin, so the column I read off its ruler was wrong; the library's own coordinates are the ones to use. (ask 10)
- A 23-digit route string was cut after NINE steps (turn 1 -> 91 = 9 actions) with an empty message line. The patch drops the rest of a key string whenever the game flushes (TERM_XTRA_FLUSH), and screen_save()/screen_load() both signal EVENT_MESSAGE_FLUSH, so any overlay (store, look mode, character sheet) ends my string. Use ONE game command per ask (the aimed walk is one command) rather than long key chains. (ask 11)
- LOOK MODE DOES NOT FLUSH (ui-knowledge.c do_cmd_look -> target_set_interactive -> target_set_interactive_aux has no screen_save), so l<direction>g should be ONE ask. The '*' targeting mode is a different story: screen_save() signals EVENT_MESSAGE_FLUSH, and the patch drops the rest of the string on a flush, so '*p<digits>g' cannot work in this build. (ask 14)
- THE WEAPONSMITH (seed 137) stocks: Rapier 104, Short Sword 150, Tulwar 204, Long Sword 300, 2 Pikes 300, 2 Long Bows (x3) 414, 20 Bolts (1d5) 1, 20 Iron Shots (1d4) 3, 30 Iron Shots (+1,+1) 5 - and NO ARROWS. The General Store is said to sell arrow/shot/bolt, so that is where a Ranger's ammunition is. A Long Bow is 414 gold, far beyond a 124-gold budget, and the Short Bow's pval 2 means an arrow does 1d4x2 = 2-8. (ask 15)
- A RUN command ('.' + direction) is ONE command and cannot be cut by a flush, unlike a digit string. Testing it here: from (19,10) the General Store tile is (19,8). (ask 17)
- GENERAL STORE PRICES (seed 137), per ITEM: Mushroom of Stoneskin 60, 11 Hard Biscuits 1, 40 Rations of Food 3, 40 Flasks of oil 3, 40 Wooden Torches (5000 turns) 6, 40 Cloaks [1,+0] 6, 40 Shovels 66, 40 Picks 176, 40 Arrows (1d4) 1, 40 Bolts (1d5) 1, 40 Iron Shots (1d4) 3. The store display shows the price of ONE (ui-store.c:632 price_item(store, obj, false, 1)), so 40 arrows is 40 gold, not 1. A torch gives a 3x3 view (cave-view.c: Rad=1); only a lantern (Rad=2, 5x5) would let me see further, and no shop here stocks one. (ask 19)
- The store's quantity prompt is textui_get_quantity: the field is NOT pre-filled, and a stray key lands in it. The first backspace deletes the whole field (src/ui-input.c, same rule as the rest prompt). A stray key in that field is not harmless: the purchase does not happen until the field parses. (ask 22)
- Both the look-mode walk and the run stop 2-3 steps short of the target in the town. run_test (player-path.c) aborts a run when the square ahead is 'interesting' (a store is), when a monster is visible, when a trap is visible, or when a visible OBJECT lies ahead - and the pathfind branch of run_step aborts on known impassable terrain or a visible object. The town has no drawn items, so the abort is a monster out of my torch radius or an object I cannot see. Rule for now: a walk may stop short; check where I am after every ask. (ask 26)
- ALCHEMY SHOP STOCK (seed 137), per item: 40 Cure Light Wounds 20, 2 Cure Serious Wounds 70, 3 Neutralize Poison 75, Restore Life Levels 400, 2 Berserk Strength 80, 9 Resist Heat 30, 14 Resist Cold 30, 40 Scrolls of Phase Door 15, 40 Scrolls of Remove Curse 100, 40 Scrolls of Word of Recall 125. NO Magic Mapping today. (ask 29)
- The down staircase of the town is at (35,2) and the game put me on it at birth, so it is a KNOWN staircase: l>g (look, jump the cursor to the nearest known down staircase, walk there) should take me back in one ask. (ask 33)
- ON DEPTH 1. Arrived on an Up staircase (the connected-stairs rule). Level feeling 1 = quietest. Light 2 = a torch, i.e. a 3x3 view, so I see only the eight squares around me: every monster will come to me, and I cannot use oil or the bow at range. (ask 40)
- LOOK MODE IS A TARGETING MODE: it has 'p' (free mode, cursor to the player), the digit keys move the cursor one tile each, and 'g' pathfinds to the cursor (ui-target.c, allow_pathfinding is true for TARGET_LOOK). So l + p + digits + g is the aimed walk that does not get cut by a flush - unlike '*' + p + digits + g, where screen_save() signals EVENT_MESSAGE_FLUSH and the patch drops the rest of the string. (ask 41)
- EXPLORATION ON A FRESH LEVEL: 'lxg' moved ONE tile (ask 41), and the aimed walk in look mode (l p <20 digits> g) moved one tile the wrong way (ask 42) - find_path's unknown-destination fallback gives a bogus short path, as minimaxflash-2 measured. The run command is the remaining fast method; it stops when it sees a monster, a trap, rubble, a door or a corner, but it walks into a monster that is outside my 3x3 torch radius. (ask 42)
- explore.py on the M map: 47 of 1716 box tiles have terrain = 3% of the level remembered, the up staircase is at box (40,5), no '>' remembered yet, and most of the dark is south-east (480 box tiles). It suggests the aimed walk '*' + 40 down-right digits + g (the cursor clamps to the level, so the aim lands in the far corner and find_path treats the unknown as passable). (ask 51)
- A run ('.'+direction) is good in CORRIDORS and useless in open rooms: run_test (player-path.c) stops the run when the squares around the direction are 'non-adjacent new directions', so in a 3-wide room a run stops after one tile. Digit strings are the way across a room, and they die at walls. (ask 54)
- CONTROLLED TEST of the digit string: four '6' keys over four known floor tiles in the room. The turn counter says how many actions happened (10 turns each), so this measures the string, not my guess. (ask 56)
- MY OWN TOOL: runs/mmx-ran-1/tools/chain.py (kept in the run, and it says so in its header). It reads the panel through the shared tools/screen/screen.py (which pads the rows, so a blank row is not a wall) and plans a chain of direction keys that ends in the dark, with the first step required to be known floor. The idea is minimaxflash-1.5's; the difference is the padded reader and the first-step rule. (ask 57)
- The panel of screen-60 shows a second large room to the south (rows 11-16, x=23-44) with a gold pile ('$') at (24,14) and two rodents ('r' = the giant white mouse: 2 hp, AC 4, 1d2, MULTIPLY, experience 1) at (34,14) and (25,15). The panel also shows the remembered level, which is how I found them: the letter 'r' is a monster and the '$' is gold. (ask 61)
- THE CUT, MEASURED: the first key of an ask is eaten whenever the previous screen ended with a prompt - a '-more-' or the 'Hit any key to continue' of the whole-level map. Ask 61 pressed M, so ask 62's first '6' went to that prompt: 9 keys sent, 8 moves. Ask 56 ended in a -more-, so ask 57's four '6' keys gave three moves. That is most of what I took for a mysterious flush. Also: a diagonal step is refused when one of its two orthogonal neighbours is a wall, which is why a chain dies beside a '%' vein. (ask 62)
- A chain and a run are different tools: the chain is cut by whatever the game does while it runs (a wall, a waking monster), the run stops on its own rules. Test: from the room's south edge the run should carry me the length of the row. (ask 66)
- THE CUT, SOLVED: player-util.c disturb() signals EVENT_INPUT_FLUSH, and the patch drops the rest of the key string on a flush. So anything that disturbs the player ends my chain: a monster noticing me, a monster hitting me, picking up gold (cmd-pickup.c:423 disturbs before every autopickup), a failed lock pick or disarm. Two awake mice in this room were disturbing me every single action, which is why the last six asks each gave exactly ONE step however long the chain was. Kill the mice (or leave them behind) and the chains work again. (ask 71)
- FOUND THE CUT: mon-attack.c, a visible monster that MISSES me also disturbs the player, and disturb() signals EVENT_INPUT_FLUSH, which the patch turns into 'drop the rest of the key string'. So the two awakened giant white mice were ending every chain after one step. Killing them (or getting away from them) is what makes long chains work. (ask 72)
- THE THROW PROMPT (seed 137): 'Throw which item? (Throwing items: a-a, / for Inven, | for Quiver, ESC)' - the throw list numbers the THROWABLES from a, not the pack letters, and here only the flasks of oil are throwable (the arrows are in the quiver, '|' for Quiver). The oil is 'a' in the throw list and 'c' in the pack. (ask 75)
- The throw works in ONE ask once the -more- is accounted for: 'v' (throw), 'a' (the oil, in the throw list's own numbering), then "'" (the closest monster), then a space to clear the "...is targeted. -more-", then the direction to throw along. One flask of oil killed a giant white mouse for 1 experience (NXT 12 -> 11). (ask 77)

Route: aim with 'l p <digits> g' in look mode instead, and let the game's own pathfinder over known ground do the walking. (ask 156)
- MEASURED: the look-mode aimed walk moved me 4 tiles (2 diagonal steps, 2 actions) - so the game's own pathfinder steps diagonally even where a digit chain would not: the chain's 4th key '3' from (33,5) to (34,6) did nothing, while the pathfinder took exactly that step. The difference is not geometry, because (33,4) is rubble and (34,6) has four floor neighbours either way. Use 'l p <digits> g' for navigation and do not trust route.py's diagonal steps. (ask 157)

The town's street is not navigable by the game's own pathfinder. (ask 160)

The most likely cause is the autopickup: cmd-pickup.c disturbs before every autopickup, and the town's floor carries goods the map does not draw, so every step onto such a square flushes the key string. - SHOPPING PLAN, seed 137, 150 gold: I buy only the Soft Leather Armour (84 gold, +8 AC, AC 2 -> 10). The town costs 1-3 tiles an ask to walk, so each extra shop is 8-18 asks of pure navigation, and my 10 flasks of oil are already worth about 30 experience (6 ants = CL2, 10 = CL4), which is as much oil as I can afford to USE in the asks I have left. Armour is the one purchase that decides whether I survive depth 2. (ask 163)
- TOWN, the long street on row 10 runs from x=4 to x=64 and the stores sit in alcoves off it; the wild dog (glyph C, the canine base) is at (39,9) and the Armoury at (38,8) is entered from (38,9) going north. (ask 165)
- FOUND THE CUT IN THE TOWN: cmd-pickup.c do_autopickup() calls disturb(p) for EVERY object on the square you step onto, and disturb() signals EVENT_INPUT_FLUSH, which this build's patch turns into 'drop the rest of the key string'. The town's floor carries goods the map does not draw, so every single step in the town ends the string: 1 tile an ask. In the dungeon most squares are empty, so chains and walks survive there. This also means the number of steps in a path IS the number of asks in a town. (ask 166)
- MEASURED: 'The scruffy little dog bites you.' - a town dog at (37,10) is adjacent to my route and its bite disturbs me, which is the second thing that cuts a town walk (the first is do_autopickup's disturb for every object on the square). HP 11/12. qwen-flash-1 died in this town to this monster. (ask 169)
- MEASURED: the bow beats the dagger against a 2 hp monster. An arrow is 1d4 x 2 = 2-8 damage, so every hit kills a scruffy little dog, and the bow's to-hit is 32 against the dog's AC 4 - about 88 per cent - where the Main Gauche is 1d5 at to-hit 18, only 60 per cent of which rolls 2 or more. One key ('h' fires the first quiver ammo at the nearest visible monster) kills the dog without the bite that a melee round would cost, because my blow happens first in the turn. (ask 170)
- MEASURED: an arrow (the message says 'Your Arrow (1d4)') left the 2 hp scruffy little dog at 1 hp - the sidebar health bar showed [*---------] and the dog fled in terror rather than dying. So the Short Bow's pval of 2 did NOT double the arrow's damage, even though player-calcs.c sets state->ammo_mult = launcher->pval and player-attack.c multiplies by it. My own RANGER page says the bow does 1d4x2 = 2-8; that is wrong, or the multiplier is not applied to this arrow. Also: the glyph '{' on the map is an ARROW lying on the floor (object.txt: graphics:{:U}), and '}' is a bow. (ask 173)

- MEASURED: the store's own message gives the pack letter of what you just bought - 'You have Soft Leather Armour [8,+0] (m).' - so I did not need to open the inventory to find it. Buying the armour leaves 66 of my 150 gold. (ask 176)
- RECALL TRICK: effect_handler_RECALL sets player->word_recall = randint0(20) + 15, and game-world.c decrements it once per player action; when it reaches 0 in the TOWN it prints 'You feel yourself yanked downwards!' and calls dungeon_change_level(player, player->recall_depth), and player_set_recall_depth() sets recall_depth = MAX(recall_depth, 1) = my deepest level = 1. So reading the Recall in the town and then resting 60 actions puts me on depth 1 in two asks, where walking to the town's staircase costs about fifteen asks at one tile an ask. (ask 178)
- The rest prompt is pre-filled with '& ' and one backspace clears the whole field (src/ui-input.c), so 'R' + backspace + '60' + Enter is a timed rest of 60 player actions; the rest count is in player actions, and the Recall's counter ticks once per player action, so 60 covers the longest possible wait of 34. (ask 179)

It also cost the Scroll of Word of Recall, which was the cheapest exit: 2 asks (read it, then rest 60) instead of about 15 asks of walking to the town's staircase at one tile an ask. The Recall fired after 26 player actions, which is inside its 15-34 range. (ask 180)
- FRESH DEPTH 1. I arrive on an up staircase at the end of a one-wide corridor that runs east (the panel shows ### / .@# / ###). The run command is the sweep tool: run_test (player-path.c) only stops a run when the squares around the direction of travel are 'non-adjacent new directions', so a run goes the length of a corridor (24 actions measured by my previous life at ask 88) and stops after one or two tiles in an open room. Staircases are placed at corridor ends (gen-util.c alloc_stairs: exactly three adjacent walls), so the corridors are where the sweep has to go. (ask 181)

So one arrow kills a 2 hp giant white mouse 75 per cent of the time, and 30 arrows are an experience engine. (ask 201)
- MEASURED: the white worm mass (monster.txt: 10 hp, AC 1, experience 2, blow CRAWL:POISON 1d2, flags WEIRD_MIND|MULTIPLY|STUPID) is at (16,3). Its poison bite is the danger, so it must be shot from range and not approached. Against AC 1 the Ranger's shooting to-hit of 32 is about 95 per cent, and 2-8 damage a shot kills 10 hp in two arrows. (ask 203)
- MEASURED: one 'h' fires the first quiver ammo at the nearest visible monster, and the shot message is followed by a -more- prompt, so the next ask must start with a space. The sidebar health bar is NOT redrawn while that prompt is up - I read a full bar after a shot that had already done 8 damage, so read the bar one ask later, not on the shot's own screen. (ask 204)
- MEASURED: the white worm mass MULTIPLIES - the first one bred a second one (a new 'w' appeared at (15,4)) the moment it was hit and woke. A breeding monster is a tax on every ask, because an awake monster near me disturbs me and cuts the chain, so it has to be killed or left far behind. (ask 205)
- MEASURED, THE COST OF A KILL: five arrows at two 10 hp white worm masses (AC 1) did not kill either, because an arrow can break on impact ('The Arrow breaks.') and the mass breeds a second copy the moment it is hit. Five arrows for two experience is a bad trade for a Ranger with 27 arrows left, so I left them and went on: a breeding monster is only worth fighting where it cannot multiply (a corridor, not a room), and even then not with arrows that break. (ask 206)
- MEASURED: after firing an arrow the game keeps the shooting command as a REPEAT, and the next command I give is fired at the nearest monster instead of being carried out - so a chain sent straight after a shot shoots instead of walking, and the shot's own -more- prompt eats the key after it. Break the repeat with a movement key before anything else, and check the map after every shot. (ask 207)
- MEASURED: the whole-level map's vertical scale is 3 level tiles a box row (display_map in ui-map.c: row = (y * map_hgt) / cave->height) and the horizontal 2.5, so the map's box is the whole 190x60-odd level, not a window. My known area is level x about 4 to 60, and I stand at about x 43, y 33 - the western middle. The dark is north-east, and the level's eastern two thirds has never been seen, so the sweep has to go east. (ask 209)
- MEASURED: a teleport trap on depth 1 moved me from the western middle of the level to its far north-east corner (the whole-level map put me at box 70,3 of 78x22, which is level x about 175, y about 9) - the whole known blob is now 116 of 1716 box tiles, 7 per cent, and 1145 of the 1598 dark boxes are south-west of me. The trap is a hazard (it disturbs, so it also cuts a key string) and an opportunity: it moved me a hundred tiles in one step. (ask 212)
- MEASURED: the whole-level map puts me at box 60,1 of 78x22, which is level x about 150 and y about 3 - the NORTH EDGE of a 190x60-odd level, with 1088 of the 1557 dark box tiles to the south-west. Coverage is 157 of 1716 box tiles, 9 per cent. The vertical scale of the map is confirmed at 3 level tiles a box row (display_map, ui-map.c: row = (y * map_hgt) / cave->height), so the map is the whole level and the dark direction is a real direction. (ask 217)
- MEASURED: the remembered map showed floor at (37,6) and the corridor at x=37 does run from row 5 down to row 13 - my chain had twelve 8s, which is NORTH, and the wall message was the corridor's north end at row 4. The keypad is 7 8 9 = up, 4 5 6 = across, 1 2 3 = down, and '8' is up and '2' is down. (ask 218)
- MEASURED: the remembered map showed floor at (45,12) and the walk refused it with 'There is a wall in the way!' - the remembered map can show floor where there is a wall, so a chain can die on a square the map says is open. Route round what the map claims. (ask 224)
- MEASURED, THE KEYPAD: 7 8 9 is NORTH, 4 5 6 is ACROSS and 1 2 3 is SOUTH. I wrote '2' where I meant north in two chains in a row and each cost an ask: the walk refused the wall and the message said so, so the mistake was cheap but not free. (ask 225)
- MEASURED: 120 gold on the floor of the room (AU 126 -> 246). Gold is only worth spending in town and I cannot get back to town cheaply from here, so from now on it is a record number and nothing else - but the piles are big, so it is still worth one ask to walk over them. (ask 226)
- MEASURED: the remembered map lies in a way that costs asks - it drew floor at squares that were walls (24,12) and (23,15), and each chain that trusted it died with 'There is a wall in the way!'. The reliable way to read the ground is tools/screen/look.py, which gives every drawn glyph as an offset from '@'. (ask 233)
- MEASURED: I stood on a dead end - walls north, east and west, floor south - and the status line said 'Open floor', so it is not a down staircase even though the shape is the one a staircase is placed on (gen-util.c alloc_stairs: exactly three adjacent walls). The status line's last field is the authority: it said 'Down staircase' when I stood on the town's and 'Up staircase' on the level's. (ask 234)
- MEASURED: an item on the floor is only NOTICED by the autopickup ('You see a Magenta Potion.') - it is not picked up, because pickup_always is off; the 'g' key is what picks it up. The flavour name says nothing about the kind: flavor_assign() in src/obj-util.c hands the flavours to the kinds at random, so 'Magenta' is not an identification. (ask 242)
- MEASURED: a trap door on depth 1 has flags TRAP|FLOOR|DOWN with effect DAMAGE 2d8 and save FEATHER (vendor/angband/lib/gamedata/trap.txt), and it is INVISIBLE until found - so walking blindly over an unknown floor can drop me a level for 2 to 16 damage, which at 12 hit points is a coin flip unless the feather save (based on INT) saves me. It is a way down without a staircase, and it costs the connected-stairs escape. (ask 246)
- MEASURED: the 'm' in the corridor is a grey mould - monster_base.txt 'mold' has glyph m, flags NEVER_MOVE and IM_POIS, and monster.txt's grey mold is 2 hp, armour class 1, experience 3, one blow SPORE:HURT:1d4, sleepiness 0. It never moves, so it cannot reach me, and its spores are released when you step NEXT to it, so an arrow from range is the safe way to take its three experience. (ask 248)
- MEASURED: shooting asks 'Target out of range by 3 squares. Fire anyway? [y/n]' - the grey mould is 13 tiles away and the bow's range is 10. So the mould is VISIBLE 13 tiles away (it is drawn on the map) even though it is out of range: a monster can be seen further than it can be shot. (ask 249)
- MEASURED: the grey mould's spore HURTS (monster.txt: blow:SPORE:HURT:1d4) - it is the grey MUSHROOM PATCH that confuses (SPORE:CONFUSE) and the yellow one that terrifies (SPORE:TERRIFY). So standing next to a grey mould costs 1d4 and nothing worse, and it never moves. The bow's range is 10 squares and the mould is 13 away, so an arrow needs me next to it. (ask 250)
- MEASURED, CORRECTING MYSELF: the Short Bow's pval of 2 does NOT multiply the arrow's damage in this build - the message says 'Your Arrow (1d4)', the grey mould (2 hp) is still alive after a hit that should have killed it, and the floating eye lost 8 hit points to two arrows (1d4 twice, both 4). So an arrow does 1d4 = 1 to 4 damage, average 2.5. My own RANGER page is right on the bow's pval and wrong about the damage; a 2 hp mouse needs one arrow 75 per cent of the time, not always. (ask 252)
- MEASURED: the grey mould died to two arrows and gave 3 experience - the sidebar's NXT went from 5 to 2, so this character is two experience short of character level 2. A grey mould is 2 hit points, armour class 1, 3 experience, never moves, and its spore only HURTS (1d4): the best target on a depth-1 level for a Ranger with a bow, and it cannot reach me. (ask 253)
- MEASURED: the two 'C' glyphs south-west are wild dogs (monster.txt: 3 hp, armour class 3, experience 1, blow BITE:HURT:1d1, speed 110, depth 1) - one experience each and a 1d1 bite, so two of them are the two experience this character needs for character level 2. There is also an unknown scroll on the floor at (3,8) and two arrows: I will NOT read the scroll, because on depth 1 a scroll is 7 per cent a Deep Descent (which drops me five levels below my deepest, to depth 6, where the worst round is 32 damage) and 3 per cent a Summon Monster, and the first use of an unknown scroll pays only its own level of experience - at most 3 here, which cannot reach character level 3 anyway. (ask 254)
- MEASURED: the second wild dog gave the last experience and the character is now LEVEL 2 - the sidebar reads LEVEL 2, HP 12/17 (the Ranger's hit die is 5, so the level gave +5) and NXT 18 (18 more experience for level 3). On depth 1 at level 2 the kills pay half or less: a wild dog (1 experience at level 1) is 0 at level 2, a grey mould 3 becomes 1. Level 3 is out of reach on depth 1 (18 more experience), which is exactly why a staircase matters: at depth 2 the experience doubles. (ask 259)
- MEASURED: the chain that was meant to walk 16 tiles east stopped after ONE tile and I had lost a hit point: the wild dog at (32,9) bit me, and a monster that attacks me - even for 1 damage - disturbs the player, which drops the rest of the key string. An awake monster next to a Ranger is a tax on every ask, not just on its own. (ask 263)

note: LOOK MODE IS A TARGETING MODE: it has 'p' (free mode, cursor to the player), the digit keys move the cursor one tile each, and 'g' pathfinds to the cursor (ui-target.c, allow_pathfinding is true for TARGET_LOOK). So l + p + digits + g is the aimed walk that does not get cut by a flush - unlike '*' + p + digits + g, where screen_save() signals EVENT_MESSAGE_FLUSH and the patch drops the rest of the string. (ask 41)

note: A run ('.'+direction) is good in CORRIDORS and useless in open rooms: run_test (player-path.c) stops the run when the squares around the direction are 'non-adjacent new directions', so in a 3-wide room a run stops after one tile. Digit strings are the way across a room, and they die at walls. (ask 54)

note: THE CUT, MEASURED: the first key of an ask is eaten whenever the previous screen ended with a prompt - a '-more-' or the 'Hit any key to continue' of the whole-level map. Ask 61 pressed M, so ask 62's first '6' went to that prompt: 9 keys sent, 8 moves. Ask 56 ended in a -more-, so ask 57's four '6' keys gave three moves. That is most of what I took for a mysterious flush. Also: a diagonal step is refused when one of its two orthogonal neighbours is a wall, which is why a chain dies beside a '%' vein. (ask 62)

note: THE CUT, SOLVED: player-util.c disturb() signals EVENT_INPUT_FLUSH, and the patch drops the rest of the key string on a flush. So anything that disturbs the player ends my chain: a monster noticing me, a monster hitting me, picking up gold (cmd-pickup.c:423 disturbs before every autopickup), a failed lock pick or disarm. Two awake mice in this room were disturbing me every single action, which is why the last six asks each gave exactly ONE step however long the chain was. Kill the mice (or leave them behind) and the chains work again. (ask 71)

note: FOUND THE CUT: mon-attack.c, a visible monster that MISSES me also disturbs the player, and disturb() signals EVENT_INPUT_FLUSH, which the patch turns into 'drop the rest of the key string'. So the two awakened giant white mice were ending every chain after one step. Killing them (or getting away from them) is what makes long chains work. (ask 72)

note: PLAN: my previous life swept depth 1 for 150 asks, reached 9% coverage and found no down staircase, so I do not repeat that. The town trip resets the level (game-world.c: on_leave_level -> prepare_next_level -> cave_free), which costs me nothing because 9% coverage bought nothing, and it buys AC (AC 2 halves the hits I take when armour takes it to ~7), 10 more flasks of oil (a giant black ant is 11 hp and 3 exp: one flask kills it, 6 exp = character level 2), 32 arrows and a heal. The Alchemist may hold a Magic Mapping scroll (65 gold; MAP_AREA in effect-handler-general.c memorises every non-floor feature in a 44x80 window, so it WOULD draw a staircase), and the Black Market is a random object of level max_depth+5..+20 at 150% of value, so it may hold one too. (ask 152)

note: FOUND THE CUT IN THE TOWN: cmd-pickup.c do_autopickup() calls disturb(p) for EVERY object on the square you step onto, and disturb() signals EVENT_INPUT_FLUSH, which this build's patch turns into 'drop the rest of the key string'. The town's floor carries goods the map does not draw, so every single step in the town ends the string: 1 tile an ask. In the dungeon most squares are empty, so chains and walks survive there. This also means the number of steps in a path IS the number of asks in a town. (ask 166)

note: RECALL TRICK: effect_handler_RECALL sets player->word_recall = randint0(20) + 15, and game-world.c decrements it once per player action; when it reaches 0 in the TOWN it prints 'You feel yourself yanked downwards!' and calls dungeon_change_level(player, player->recall_depth), and player_set_recall_depth() sets recall_depth = MAX(recall_depth, 1) = my deepest level = 1. So reading the Recall in the town and then resting 60 actions puts me on depth 1 in two asks, where walking to the town's staircase costs about fifteen asks at one tile an ask. (ask 178)

note: FRESH DEPTH 1. I arrive on an up staircase at the end of a one-wide corridor that runs east (the panel shows ### / .@# / ###). The run command is the sweep tool: run_test (player-path.c) only stops a run when the squares around the direction of travel are 'non-adjacent new directions', so a run goes the length of a corridor (24 actions measured by my previous life at ask 88) and stops after one or two tiles in an open room. Staircases are placed at corridor ends (gen-util.c alloc_stairs: exactly three adjacent walls), so the corridors are where the sweep has to go. (ask 181)
